## 👤 User

Read DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md, SURFACE_FITTING.md, and README.md for context.

I want to explore a radical change to surface fitting approach. The idea would be to start with a single seed face, and expand it, changing the fitting strategy as needed to adapt to changes in the surface. For example, for a seed face on a sphere, initially a plan would fit the single face. Then an adjacent face would require a cylinder to fit. Adding one more face might make a cone a better fit. Adding an additional face might promote the fit to sphere. The idea is to use the simplest fit which fits the patch to within vertex and surface tolerances.

I want to explore options for the fitting math. Would it make sense to use a general quadratic fitter, and when there's insufficient support to pin down the result, default to the simplest surface that fits? How would we handle tori - upgrade to quartic, or use a torus-specific fitter?

Do some searching to see if there's some existing applicable literature on these topics. Then think about what is likely to work best and present me some options.

1	# Brepper - STL to STEP Converter
2	
3	Convert triangulated STL meshes from CAD exports into parametric STEP files with fitted analytic and freeform surfaces.
4	
5	## Usage
6	
7	\`\`\`
8	brepper - Convert STL mesh to STEP with fitted surfaces
9	
10	USAGE:
11	    brepper [OPTIONS] <input.stl> [-o <output.step>]
12	
13	REQUIRED:
14	    <input.stl>              Input STL file (binary or ASCII)
15	
16	GENERAL OPTIONS:
17	    -o <step>, --output=<step>  Output STEP file
18	    --stl-units=<units>      Units used by the STL file (mm, cm, m, in, ft, um; default: mm)
19	    --step-units=<units>     Units to use in exported STEP file (default: mm)
20	    --compare=<step>         STEP file to compare to at each step
21	    --reference-report       Score the mesh and our segmentation against the --compare
22	                             reference: facet-normal exactness per surface type, closed-form
23	                             initializer error, the seam assumption, and over/under-segmentation.
24	                             Diagnostics only. Requires --compare.
25	    --invariant-report       Score the DEVELOPMENT_PLAN "Invariants" against the --compare
26	                             reference: whether the fitting requirement is satisfiable, whether
27	                             an angular tolerance can separate adjacent surfaces, whether a wrong
28	                             surface type also explains a true support, and whether growth from
29	                             different seeds converges on the same surface. Runs after stage 2.2.
30	                             Diagnostics only. Requires --compare.
31	    --vertex-tolerance=<value>  Fitting tolerance in STL units (default: 5e-5)
32	    --surface-tolerance=<value> Surface-to-face offset tolerance in STL units (default: 0.4)
33	    --angular-tolerance=<deg>   Max dihedral angle between adjacent triangles on the same surface (default: 25)
34	    -v, --verbose[=LEVEL]    Verbosity level: 1 = stage summaries, 2 = per-hypothesis details,
35	                             3 = commit details/stats/profiles, 4 = per-seed details, 5 = BFS face trace
36	    -q, --quiet              Suppress non-error output
37	    --strict-mesh            Fail on mesh defects instead of repairing them (dropping
38	                             duplicate-vertex and coincident faces, stitching T-junction
39	                             slivers, unifying winding, reorienting inverted shells)
40	    --debug                  Enable debug output and intermediate files
41	    --stage=<stage>          Stop after stage, e.g. 2.2 (default: 4.1). --stage=2 stops after all of stage2
42	                             Stage 2 sub-stages (partition-then-classify rewrite): 2.1 sharp-edge
43	                             classification + jet prior, 2.2 smooth-region extraction, 2.3 per-region
44	                             surface ladder, 2.4 resolution & repair, 2.5 selected surfaces
45	    --viz-stages=<stages>    Open interactive 3D visualization at specified stages (e.g. 2.1,2.2)
46	    --viz-stages-level=<N>   Viz detail level: 1 = committed hypotheses (default), 2 = region colors
47	    --viz-seeds              Interactively visualize how SEED SETS grow, against the --compare
48	                             ground truth. Stops on each facet for which every conforming seed
49	                             set fails, and names the validation clause that refused it.
50	                             Requires --compare. See "Seed-growth visualization" below.
51	    --viz-seed-show=<what>   Which seed sets to stop on: failing (default), all, covering
52	    --viz-seed-sizes=<list>  Facet counts the seed generators may use; only the maximum
53	                             is read, as the bound on chain length (default 2,3,4 — the
54	                             sizes the DEVELOPMENT_PLAN Invariants section specifies)
55	    --viz-seed-samples=<N>   Anchor facets sampled per reference face (default 12)
56	    --viz-seed-max-attempts=<N>  Conforming seed sets tried per anchor facet (default 12)
57	    --viz-seed-spec-only     Try only the seed patterns the Invariants section names, without
58	                             the grown-ball fallback — shows where those rules alone fall short
59	    --viz-seed-coplanar-clause   Enforce the section's "no more than three coplanar" clause
60	                             (measured: it rejects every seed containing a fused quad)
61	    --viz-seed-confine-region    Confine seed growth to the stage-2.1 smooth region instead of
62	                             letting validation be the only limit
63	\`\`\`
64	
65	### Examples
66	
67	\`\`\`bash
68	# Full conversion
69	./target/debug/brepper input.stl -o output.step
70	
71	# Just load and validate mesh (stage 1.2)
72	./target/debug/brepper input.stl --stage 1.2 -v
73	
74	# Compare mesh against reference STEP at each stage
75	./target/debug/brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --stage 1 -v
76	
77	# Score the mesh against ground truth read from the reference (see src/reference.rs)
78	./target/debug/brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --stage 1 --reference-report -v
79	
80	# Score the Invariants section's clauses against that ground truth
81	./target/release/brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --stage 2.2 --invariant-report -v
82	
83	# Watch which seed sets fail to grow to their surface, and why
84	./target/release/brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --stage 2.2 --viz-seeds -v
85	\`\`\`
86	
87	### Reference scoring (\`--reference-report\`)
88	
89	With a \`--compare\` reference, brepper can label every mesh face with the
90	reference face it came from — recovered from the distance computation the
91	compare already performs — and then score three things against that ground
92	truth. All of it is diagnostics: nothing downstream reads it.
93	
94	At **stage 1**, properties of the mesh alone (so they are still measurable on
95	models where stage 2 fails):
96	
97	- **Facet-normal exactness** per reference surface type: the angle between the
98	  mesh facet normal and the true surface normal. Analytic faces use a closed
99	  form; B-spline, Bezier, revolution, extrusion and offset faces are evaluated
100	  through OCCT at the footpoint, and facets at a pole or seam — where the normal
101	  does not exist — are excluded rather than scored.
102	- **Closed-form initializer error**: the cylinder axis from \`n₁ × n₂\`, the
103	  sphere centre from circumcenter normal lines, and the torus
104	  meridian-coplanarity residual, each against the reference's own parameters,
105	  reported with the conditioning that governs them.
106	- **The seam assumption**: how often a facet's own interior disagrees about
107	  which reference face it belongs to.
108	
109	At the end of **stage 2**, our decomposition against the reference's:
110	
111	- **\`ref coverage\`** — 1.0 when no reference face was split across several of
112	  our surfaces (no over-segmentation).
113	- **\`surface purity\`** — 1.0 when none of our surfaces spans several reference
114	  faces (no merging).
115	
116	Both are area-weighted and threshold-free. They catch what the distance and
117	volume gates cannot: a 9-surface decomposition of a 26-surface part passes every
118	existing gate but scores \`surface purity 0.2201\`.
119	
120	### Invariant scoring (\`--invariant-report\`)
121	
122	Scores the clauses of DEVELOPMENT_PLAN's **Invariants** section against the same
123	ground truth. Also diagnostics only, and it runs after **stage 2.2** rather than at
124	the end of stage 2, so it still works on models whose decomposition crashes or
125	times out. One TSV row per reference face on stdout, a per-model summary on stderr.
126	
127	\`\`\`bash
128	./target/release/brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --stage 2.2 --invariant-report -v
129	\`\`\`
130	
131	Five families, one per clause:
132	
133	- **F — is the fitting requirement satisfiable?** With the reference's *own* exact
134	  parameters, how far off it the facets' vertices, edge midpoints, quad diagonals,
135	  centroids and interiors actually land. This asks whether a clause can be met at
136	  all before asking whether the algorithm meets it, and it is what showed that
137	  facet centroids are not a bound on the whole facet while edge midpoints are.
138	- **A — can an angular tolerance separate adjacent surfaces?** The largest dihedral
139	  *inside* a reference face against the smallest *across* its boundary, plus the
140	  count of edges stage 2.1 calls sharp strictly inside a true surface.
141	- **I — does a wrong surface type also explain a true support?** All four analytic
142	  types fitted to each reference face's own facets, with residuals in float32
143	  quanta.
144	- **S — seed independence.** Growth run from many seeds inside one true surface:
145	  how many recover it, how many distinct answers come back, and whether a failure
146	  leaked past the true boundary or stopped short of it.
147	- **G — growth independence.** The flood re-run with the neighbour iteration
148	  reversed, and the refit fixpoint restarted from the whole true face set.
149	
150	Corpus sweep and aggregation:
151	
152	\`\`\`bash
153	./tools/invariants.sh --out tmp/inv.tsv --jobs 8 && python3 tools/inv_analyze.py tmp/inv.tsv
154	\`\`\`
155	
156	\`tools/invariants.sh\` reuses \`tools/sweep.sh\`'s model/reference pairing, so the two
157	instruments always cover the same corpus.
158	
159	Env-gated probes on the same path, for when a single fit misbehaves:
160	\`BREPPER_INV_FITDUMP=1\` prints the reference's own parameters beside the fitted
161	ones for any seed that fails admission, with a verdict on whether the failure is
162	non-identifiability or non-convergence; \`BREPPER_INV_BASIN=1\` sweeps the basin of
163	attraction of grow-with-refit; \`BREPPER_INV_PATCHSIZE=1\` measures fit accuracy
164	against support size.
165	
166	### Seed-growth visualization (\`--viz-seeds\`)
167	
168	Opens the 3D window and walks **anchor facets**, stopping on each one for which
169	*every* conforming seed set fails to grow to its surface. Requires \`--compare\`.
170	
171	\`\`\`bash
172	./target/release/brepper tests/ccad/generated/simple_cone.stl   --compare tests/ccad/generated/simple_cone.step --stage 2.2   --viz-seeds --viz-seed-spec-only --viz-seed-samples 12 -v
173	\`\`\`
174	
175	| colour | meaning |
176	|--------|---------|
177	| white | the anchor facet |
178	| green | the rest of the seed set |
179	| blue | correctly covered |
180	| red | overshoot — grown past the true boundary |
181	| orange | true facets that **blocked** further growth |
182	| yellow | true facets missed for some other reason |
183	
184	Controls are the same as \`--viz-stages\`: \`Space\` next seed set, \`Shift+Space\` next
185	reference face, \`Q\` quit. The status line names the seed shape, how far the seed
186	sits from its own fit, and the diagnosis — which validation clause refused what,
187	since \`face_validates\` is a conjunction of vertex distance, centroid distance and
188	orientation coherence and naming only the first is misleading.
189	
190	\`BREPPER_VIZ_SEEDS_TEXT=1\` prints the same diagnoses with no window, which is what
191	the corpus catalogues are built from.
192	
193	See [DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md](DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md) for full documentation.
194	
195	### Tolerance Settings
196	
197	#### Surface Tolerance
198	
199	This is how far the surface of a mesh triangle is allowed to deviate from the surface it is approximating.
200	
201	Onshape calls this "Chordal tolerance (mm)", and the preset values are:
202	- Coarse: 0.24 mm
203	- Medium: 0.12 mm
204	- Fine: 0.06 mm
205	
206	Fusion 360 calls this "Surface Deviation", and the preset values seem to vary based on model size:
207	- Low: 1x
208	- Medium: 2.5x
209	- High: 8x
210	
211	#### Angular Tolerance
212	
213	This is the maximum angle between adjacent triangle faces that approximate a smooth surface.
214	
215	Onshape calls this "Angular deviation (deg)", and the present values are:
216	- Coarse: 12.5 deg
217	- Medium: 6.25 deg
218	- Fine: 2.5 deg
219	
220	Fusion 360 calls this "Normal Deviation", and the preset values are:
221	- Low: 30.0
222	- Medium: 15.0
223	- High: 10.0
224	In theory "Normal Deviation" should be the maximum angular error of the face relative to the surface normal, but in practice it seems to result in the given maximum angular deviation.
225	
226	### OCCT-Generated Test Models
227	
228	Models in \`tests/occt/\` and \`tests/nurbs/\` are generated directly via OCCT by
229	the \`gen-test-models\` binary (\`./scripts/generate_occt_models.sh\`) — used for
230	models that ccad cannot express. \`tests/occt/\` holds analytic models such as
231	\`hollow_cube\` (a solid with a fully enclosed internal void, for multi-shell
232	solid support). \`tests/nurbs/\` holds freeform models (exact B-spline patches,
233	conic/B-spline extrusions) exercising the stage 2.7 NURBS fallback; all 8
234	pass full \`--compare\` — see \`tests/nurbs/README.md\`. The generated STL/STEP pairs are committed.
235	
236	### Generating Test Models
237	
238	Some tests use models generated via [CodeCAD](https://codecad.xyz/). The generated STL/STEP files are committed to the repo, so CodeCAD is not required to run tests.
239	
240	To add or modify these models:
241	1. Install CodeCAD
242	2. Edit files in \`tests/ccad/parts/\` — see \`tests/ccad/README.md\` for primitive conventions and placement rules
243	3. Run \`./tests/ccad/generate_models.sh\`
244	
245	**Important:** \`box(w,d,h)\` places a corner at the origin, not centered. Use \`center_xyz()\` to center. Each part file includes analytical surface area and volume in its header comments.
246	
247	### Interactive Debugging Visualization
248	
249	Use \`--viz-stages\` to open a 3D window and step through BFS hypothesis deduction interactively.
250	Use \`--viz-stages-level\` to control how often the visualization pauses:
251	- Level 1 (default): Pauses at each committed hypothesis
252	- Level 2: Also pauses at each accepted seed before BFS expansion
253	- Level 3: Also pauses at each face accepted during BFS expansion
254	
255	\`\`\`bash
256	# Visualize planar and cylindrical hypothesis stages (committed hypotheses only)
257	brepper input.stl --viz-stages=2.1,2.2
258	
259	# Visualize with seed-level detail
260	brepper input.stl --viz-stages=2.2 --viz-stages-level=2
261	
262	# Visualize all stage 2 hypothesis types with STEP comparison overlay
263	brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --viz-stages=2.1,2.2,2.3
264	\`\`\`
265	
266	At each BFS step, the window shows the STL mesh with highlighted faces: seed faces in green, previously accepted faces in blue, the just-accepted face in dark blue, and BFS queue frontier edges in red. Cylindrical and spherical hypotheses also display translucent geometry overlays. When \`--compare\` is provided, the STEP surface is shown at 35% opacity for reference.
267	
268	| Action | Control |
269	|--------|:--------|
270	| Next step | \`Space\` |
271	| Skip to next seed/hypothesis | \`Shift+Space\` |
272	| Skip to face seed | \`Click\` on a face |
273	| Quit visualization | \`Q\` |
274	
275	Supported stages: \`2.1\` (sharp edges + jet prior), \`2.2\` (smooth regions), \`3.3\` (edge curves), \`3.4\` (faces), \`3.5\` (shells), \`3.6\` (solids). (The old per-type BFS viz stages are retired with the stage-2 rewrite.)
276	
277	\`--viz-seeds\` opens the same window on a different question — how seed sets grow rather than how the pipeline proceeds — and is documented under [Seed-growth visualization](#seed-growth-visualization---viz-seeds) above.
278	
279	
280	## Viewer
281	
282	\`viewer\` is an interactive 3D viewer for STL and STEP files.
283	
284	\`\`\`
285	USAGE:
286	    viewer [OPTIONS] <file>...
287	
288	ARGUMENTS:
289	    <file>...   One or more STL or STEP files to view (detected by extension)
290	
291	OPTIONS:
292	    --deflection <value>          Linear deflection for STEP tessellation (default: 0.1)
293	    --angular-deflection <value>  Angular deflection for STEP tessellation in degrees (default: 28.6)
294	\`\`\`
295	
296	Multiple files can be loaded simultaneously; each is rendered in a distinct color.
297	
298	### Examples
299	
300	\`\`\`bash
301	# View a single STEP file
302	./target/release/viewer part.step
303	
304	# View a STEP and an STL side by side
305	./target/release/viewer reference.step output.stl
306	
307	# Finer tessellation for curved surfaces
308	./target/release/viewer part.step --deflection 0.01 --angular-deflection 5.7
309	\`\`\`
310	
311	### Controls
312	
313	| Action | Control |
314	|--------|:--------|
315	| Rotate (around model center) | Left-click + drag |
316	| Pan | Right-click + drag |
317	| Zoom (at cursor) | Scroll wheel |
318	| Quit | \`q\` or \`Esc\` |
319	| Toggle perspective/orthographic | \`p\` |
320	| Toggle wireframe edges | \`e\` |
321	| Toggle soft (mesh) edges | \`Shift+E\` |
322	| Toggle solid faces | \`s\` |
323	| Toggle hidden-edge removal | \`h\` |
324	
325	Rendering uses PBR shading with ambient and two directional lights. Sharp feature edges (boundary edges and edges where the dihedral angle exceeds ~45°) are drawn as a solid wireframe overlay. Softer mesh edges (dihedral angle below ~45°) are drawn at 50% opacity and can be toggled independently with \`Shift+E\`. Zooming targets the point under the mouse cursor.
326

347	- All STL/STEP pairs pass \`--compare\` validation at stages 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, and 2.6
348	- Deduced cylinder parameters (axis direction, axis position, radius) match STEP cylinder parameters for all cylindrical test models
349	- Deduced sphere parameters (center, radius) match STEP sphere parameters for all spherical test models

All STL/STEP pairs pass \`--compare\` validation at stage 3.4
359	- Shell construction (stage 3.5) assembles faces into shells directly from the shared-edge topology (closed by construction; no sewing) and produces correctly oriented shells. All STL/STEP pairs pass \`--compare\` validation
360	- Solid construction (stage 3.6) creates solids from shells via \`ShapeFix_Solid::SolidFromShell\`. Volume comparison against STEP reference and \`BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape\` distance checks pass for all test models

1	# STL to STEP Conversion Utility - Development Plan
2	
3	## Project Overview
4	
5	**brepper** (B-Rep from Mesh) - A utility to convert triangulated STL meshes from CAD exports into parametric STEP files with fitted analytic and freeform surfaces.
6	
7	## Design Principles
8	
9	The stage 2 architecture was rewritten twice: from the fit-driven BFS
10	hypothesis pipeline to partition-then-classify (2026-07-17), and then —
11	after the fit-free curvature-jump classifier was measured to be
12	unsalvageable (2026-07-20, see the Stage-2 Retrospective's Resolution) —
13	to the current **sharp-partition + scoped fit competition** design. These
14	principles drive it; retired mechanisms and the reasons they died are in
15	[Abandoned Approaches](#abandoned-approaches).
16	
17	1. **Exactness is the superpower.** Mesh vertices lie on the true CAD
18	   surfaces within numerical epsilon. A candidate surface either validates
19	   every claimed vertex within \`--vertex-tolerance\` or the claim is wrong —
20	   validation is decisive, not statistical. We are not solving the
21	   fit-primitives-in-noisy-scans problem the literature (RANSAC lineage)
22	   addresses.
23	2. **The answer's boundaries are already in the mesh.** CAD tessellators emit
24	   seam edges along B-rep face boundaries — *including tangent junctions*
25	   (verified 2026-07-17 on the nosecone cone–sphere tangency: 0 of 1572 STL
26	   triangles straddle the tangency ring; 104 vertices lie exactly on it). So
27	   the true segmentation is a partition of the mesh face graph whose region
28	   boundaries are a subset of mesh edges, and every region is connected.
29	   This is architectural rather than lucky, and it is worth knowing why,
30	   because the whole pipeline rests on it — see **Mesh provenance and the seam
31	   assumption** below.
32	3. **Partition by what is certain; decompose the rest by fit competition.**
33	   Sharp dihedral edges are exact and cheap — they partition the mesh into
34	   smooth regions with no calibration (measured: every sharp-edged corpus
35	   model segments perfectly by them alone). Tangent junctions inside a
36	   smooth region are NOT reliably detectable fit-free (measured: no
37	   threshold on local curvature differences separates real junctions from
38	   real curvature gradients — the classes overlap in that statistic), so
39	   they are found by fit competition: surfaces fitted from seeds compete on
40	   validated area, and the exact vertex test places the boundary.
41	4. **Curvature allocates effort; fitting decides geometry.** The per-face
42	   jet curvature field orders seeds, prunes and orders fit attempts, seeds
43	   initial parameters, and gates expensive refits — and never decides
44	   membership or boundaries. Every curvature error converts to wasted or
45	   saved time, never to wrong output.
46	5. **Repair by validation, locally.** Remaining mistakes surface as
47	   validation failures and are repaired by local fit-validated operations —
48	   resolve multi-claimed junction rows to the better-fitting side, merge
49	   fragments a neighbor's surface explains, relabel ±1-row boundary error.
50	   No global fix-up passes with chordal slop.
51	6. **Invariants are enforced, not assumed.** Before stage 3: every mesh face
52	   belongs to exactly one region; every region is mesh-connected; every
53	   region's surface validates its own faces; results are deterministic.
54	   (The abandoned pipeline's greedy selection violated connectivity —
55	   3DBenchy face 5841 reached stage 3.4 claiming three disconnected mesh
56	   regions.) These are the *coverage* invariants and they are asserted every
57	   run; the **Invariants** section below states the per-surface and per-seed
58	   ones, which are measured rather than asserted, and says how well each holds.
59	
60	## Assumptions
61	
62	- Assumes the vertices of the mesh lie precisely on the CAD surfaces, modulo numerical error/stability.
63	- Assumes transitions between shapes always have a seam — verified to hold
64	  even for tangent transitions (see Design Principles #2 and **Mesh
65	  provenance** below). This holds for every B-rep CAD tessellator; it does
66	  *not* carry information for mesh-native sources such as OpenSCAD, which are
67	  a known, deliberately unhandled gap.
68	- Assumes manifold mesh (each edge shared by exactly 2 faces).
69	- Assumes consistent face orientation (all normals point outward).
70	
71	Fails noisily if any of the assumptions are violated, after stage 1.2's
72	repair pass has fixed what can be fixed without moving a vertex
73	(\`--strict-mesh\` disables repair).
74	
75	### Mesh provenance and the seam assumption
76	
77	Design Principle #2 — that a B-rep face boundary is always a chain of mesh
78	edges, tangent or not — is load-bearing for the entire pipeline, so this
79	records why it holds and where it stops holding. (Researched 2026-07-28.)
80	
81	**Why it holds: every B-rep faceter is edge-first.** They all discretize each
82	edge *once*, shared by both adjacent faces, and then fill each face's
83	parametric domain out to those fixed boundary polylines. This is not a
84	stylistic choice a vendor might change — it is how watertightness is
85	guaranteed. OCCT states it outright: "At the first step all edges from a face
86	are discretized… At the second step, the faces are tessellated", with "edges
87	tessellation data … used to form contours of target faces and passed as input
88	to the triangulation algorithm" and "each face is processed separately". ACIS
89	is documented as "maintaining the edge consistency between adjacent faces";
90	Parasolid's vertex-matching machinery exists so that neighbouring face meshes
91	meet exactly. A tangent edge is an edge in the *topology*, so it is discretized
92	like any other and no triangle can straddle it.
93	
94	**What the likely sources are.** For models destined for STL and print-sharing
95	sites:
96	
97	| Tool | Kernel | Faceter |
98	|------|--------|---------|
99	| Fusion 360, Inventor | ShapeManager (Autodesk's ACIS fork) | quad-tree gridding |
100	| SolidWorks, Onshape, Solid Edge, Shapr3D, Plasticity | Parasolid | per-face, vendor |
101	| FreeCAD | Open CASCADE | **Delaunay** (Watson) in UV space |
102	| Rhino | own | regular quad grid + recursive subdivision |
103	| Tinkercad, SketchUp, Blender, OpenSCAD | none — mesh/CSG native | n/a |
104	
105	Note the corpus is Onshape-heavy (Parasolid) plus CodeCAD, so it is *not*
106	representative of the faceter mix in the wild.
107	
108	**Delaunay does occur, and the assumption survives it.** FreeCAD — the
109	fastest-growing free option, and the same mesher brepper itself uses — meshes
110	with OCCT's \`BRepMesh\`, which is genuinely Delaunay. But it is a *constrained*
111	Delaunay whose constraints are the pre-discretized face boundaries, so seams
112	are preserved exactly as elsewhere. What differs between faceters is the
113	*interior* pattern: grid-based faceters (ACIS, Rhino, Parasolid) leave a
114	strongly structured, anisotropic mesh, Delaunay much less so. The practical
115	consequence is that **boundary preservation is dependable across all of them,
116	while sampling density and anisotropy are corroborating evidence only** — worth
117	remembering if the tessellation layout is ever used as a signal (it is not
118	today; stage 2 uses only dihedral angle and the curvature jet).
119	
120	**OpenSCAD and other mesh-native sources: a known gap, not handled.** These
121	have no B-rep at any stage — \`cylinder()\` is a prism and \`sphere()\` a
122	polyhedron from the moment they are created, with CSG run on polyhedra (CGAL,
123	or the newer Manifold backend). Consequences:
124	
125	- *The seam assumption holds trivially and uselessly.* Every facet is planar,
126	  so no triangle can span an orientation change — a stronger guarantee than any
127	  B-rep faceter gives. But **every** edge is a facet boundary, so nothing
128	  distinguishes an intended face boundary from a discretization step. The seam
129	  carries no information; only the curvature jump does (a constant dihedral
130	  through a fillet, dropping to 0° on the flat it meets).
131	- *There are no tangencies.* What was meant as a C¹ junction becomes a finite
132	  dihedral of one discretization step. OpenSCAD's fragment rule is
133	  \`n = ceil(max(min(360/$fa, r*2*PI/$fs), 5))\` with defaults \`$fa = 12\`,
134	  \`$fs = 2\`, so \`n\` caps at 30 and *every* curve carries a dihedral of at least
135	  12°.
136	- *Our \`--angular-tolerance\` of 25° is therefore the arbiter*, with a sharp
137	  crossover: \`$fn ≥ 15\` — or, on defaults, radius ≳ 4.5 mm, where
138	  \`ceil(πr) ≥ 15\` — reads as smooth and gets an analytic fit, while \`$fn ≤ 14\`
139	  reads as sharp and stays a prism. A \`$fn = 6\` hex nut correctly stays
140	  hexagonal; the near-ubiquitous \`$fn = 100\` reads as smooth.
141	- *Where a fit is attempted it will succeed easily.* OpenSCAD **inscribes** its
142	  polygons — the reason holes print undersized — so a prism's vertices lie
143	  exactly on the ideal cylinder of radius r, and vertex validation passes at
144	  ~zero residual. The binding check is the centroid one against
145	  \`--surface-tolerance\`: the sagitta is \`r(1 − cos(180/n))\`, which is 0.11 mm
146	  for r = 20, n = 30 (passes 0.4) but 0.55 mm for r = 100, n = 30 (fails, so
147	  the surface degrades to per-facet planes). Large-radius curves at default
148	  resolution are thus the expected failure mode.
149	- *The ambiguity is genuine, not a defect.* A 15-gon prism and a coarsely
150	  tessellated cylinder are the same mesh; nothing in it says which the designer
151	  meant. \`--angular-tolerance\` decides, which makes it a policy dial here
152	  rather than a measurement.
153	
154	We are not addressing this yet. When we do, the likely shape is a flag marking
155	the input as faceted-by-construction, or auto-detection — see TODO.md. Two
156	signals look promising for detection: an OpenSCAD revolution has a *constant*
157	dihedral of exactly 360/n around the whole curve (a B-rep tessellation's steps
158	vary and its density changes at seams), and its vertices sit exactly on the
159	circumscribed circle rather than straddling the true surface.
160	
161	## Invariants
162	
163	The point of these is consistency: the surfaces recovered from a mesh should be a
164	function of the mesh, not of facet ordering, seed choice, or traversal order.
165	They are stated as near-invariants because they are asked of a float32 mesh, so
166	"holds" means "holds to a bound derived from the mesh's own precision".
167	
168	**All of them have now been measured** against \`--compare\` ground truth over 147
169	models and 2303 reference faces (\`--invariant-report\`, see below). Every clause
170	carries its measured status. Where a clause was refuted the refutation is kept
171	rather than deleted, because several of the refuted forms are the obvious ones and
172	will otherwise be re-derived.
173	
174	### Surface Fitting Requirement
175	
176	One rule for every analytic type and for NURBS, replacing the per-type variants
177	this section used to carry:
178	
179	* **All vertices within \`--vertex-tolerance\` of the surface.**
180	* **All edge midpoints within ¾ · \`--surface-tolerance\`** — the edges of a
181	  *triangulation* of the facet, which for a fused quad means its two diagonals as
182	  well as its four sides.
183	* **No two adjacent facets of one surface separated by more than
184	  \`--angular-tolerance\`** — as a post-hoc consistency check only, never as a
185	  membership rule. See Surface Identification.
186	
187	**Why edge midpoints, and why the ¾.** Write the facet's deviation from the
188	surface as a height function vanishing at the vertices. To second order it is a
189	quadratic, and in barycentric coordinates
190	\`e(λ) = −Σ_{i<j} λᵢλⱼ · (pᵢ−pⱼ)ᵀH(pᵢ−pⱼ)\`. At the midpoint of edge \`ij\` that is
191	\`|qᵢⱼ|/8\`; maximised over the simplex it is at most \`max|q|/6\`. So
192	
193	> **max over the facet ∈ [M, (4/3)·M]**, where \`M\` is the largest edge-midpoint
194	> deviation.
195	
196	Checking edge midpoints and allowing ¾ of the tolerance is therefore a *bound* on
197	the whole facet, not a proxy for it — which answers the two TODOs this section
198	used to carry about sampling facet interiors, including for NURBS. Cost is about
199	1.5 evaluations per triangle, since each interior mesh edge is shared.
200	
201	Measured over 895 non-degenerate reference faces, ratio of the true whole-facet
202	maximum to each candidate probe:
203	
204	| probe | p95 | max | over 4/3 |
205	|---|---|---|---|
206	| boundary edge midpoints only | 1.742 | 1.999 | 63 of 895 |
207	| **edge midpoints + quad diagonals** | **1.012** | **1.497** | **1 of 895** |
208	| facet centroid | 1.384 | 2.239 | — |
209	
210	**Facet centroids are not a bound and are dropped.** The measured worst case,
211	2.239, matches the derived 9/4 for a needle triangle to three digits; and where
212	the curvature is indefinite (a saddle, a torus's inner equator) the centroid can
213	read *exactly zero* while the facet is off by the full sag. 23 corpus faces read
214	under a quarter of their edge-midpoint maximum. The single face that exceeds 4/3
215	with diagonals included is a 16-facet "plane" on a wild-corpus model that is
216	probably not planar.
217	
218	**The vertex clause holds at the mesh's own precision, not at a fixed number.**
219	On verified synthetic ground truth: plane p95 0.64 quanta, cylinder 1.22, cone
220	1.22, sphere 1.49, torus 1.45; worst case ~5 quanta. But two true cylinders on
221	\`stepped_block_toroidal_fillets_fine\` sit at 8.8 and 8.5 quanta = 7.7e-5 mm,
222	**outside the 5e-5 default**, so at today's tolerance the requirement is
223	unsatisfiable on those two surfaces and no algorithm can recover them. State the
224	bar in float32 position bounds (Phase 0.6 supplies the bound).
225	
226	**\`--surface-tolerance\` is barely non-binding.** The true worst facet sag over the
227	corpus is 0.325 mm against the 0.4 mm default — 1.23× margin — while the p50 is
228	2.2e-6 mm. Five orders of magnitude of spread under one constant; see Phase 3.2
229	for the per-face alternative.
230	
```
</details>

```
231	### Surface Seed Requirements
232	
233	A surface is discovered by growing a **seed set** — a set of facets that is the
234	entire initial fit support — under validation, refitting as it grows. There is no
235	separate unvalidated "patch": the seed set is what the first fit sees.
236	
237	A **facet chain** is a set of facets forming a path in the dual graph: each facet
238	shares an edge with the next, and each has at most two neighbours within the set.
239	(This section previously called it a "non-contiguous facet chain", which
240	contradicted its own definition — a set whose members share edges is contiguous.)
241	
242	* **Plane:** any facet of the surface. *Measured: 99.9% of plane facets have a
243	  working seed; 98.7% of plane faces have one for every facet.*
244	* **Cylinder:** a facet chain of at most 4 facets with **exactly 6 distinct
245	  vertices**. Measured, exactly three shapes reach 6 — two quads, four triangles,
246	  or two triangles and a quad — and the rule works: **94.1%** of cylinder facets
247	  have a working seed. Two quads on a cylinder give 6 vertices against 5 DOF, so
248	  there is one redundant constraint, which is what pins the right root.
249	* **Cone:** the same chain, or three triangular facets sharing one vertex (the
250	  candidate apex). The apex alternative earns its place — it is the *least*
251	  working rule for 21% of cone facets, cases the chain cannot reach. But the cone
252	  is the weak type: **49.4%** of cone facets have a working seed under these
253	  rules, and 22% have none at any support size up to 48 facets. The cause is the
254	  fitter, not the rule — see Surface Identification.
255	* **Sphere:** the seed facet plus every facet sharing an edge with it (and the
256	  same ring taken about each of its neighbours). *Measured: **100.0%** of sphere
257	  facets and **100.0%** of sphere faces. This rule is exactly right as written.*
258	* **Torus:** the closed ring of facets about one vertex — its **vertex star** —
259	  grown outward while the seed set's own fit does not yet validate the whole seed
260	  set. Typically 4–12 facets, occasionally up to 32.
261	* **NURBS:** fallback for the connected leftovers nothing analytic explains.
262	
263	**Why a torus needs a star rather than a chain.** A torus's normal lines cross the
264	axis at a point that *slides with the tube angle*, so a seed confined to one tube
265	angle cannot pin the axis — and two quads along the major direction is exactly
266	that, which is why a 6-vertex chain is under-determined for a torus. A vertex
267	star wraps a grid node, so it spans both parametric directions by construction.
268	Measured, the least support that works per torus facet: bare star 40%, the union
269	of the anchor's own vertex stars a further 25%, and the rest need 8–48 facets,
270	with 0.8% never working. Requiring the ring to **close** matters: complete stars
271	win 40% of facets, boundary-cut ones 4%.
272	
273	**The "no more than three coplanar" clause is deleted.** It was aimed at a real
274	degeneracy — a fan of facets sharing a vertex, which on a cylinder leaves the apex
275	free along a line — but as a detector it is useless and as a filter it is
276	destructive. Measured over 27 984 six-vertex chains: it rejects **85.3%** of the
277	chains that produce a good axis while catching only 39.8% of those that produce a
278	bad one. Worse, a fused quad is coplanar *by construction* — that is why it was
279	fused — so the clause rejects, by definition, every seed containing one: 99.7% of
280	2-quad chains and 92.4% of 2-triangle-plus-quad chains. And four vertices spanning
281	two rulings of a cylinder lie on two parallel lines, hence in a plane, so it also
282	bites the ruling-aligned triangle strips that Phase 1.0 measured as the *good*
283	ones. Excluding only the wholly-coplanar chains changes the worst axis error not
284	at all (90° either way).
285	
286	**The "not effectively planar" clause is deleted.** A large-radius cylinder that
287	validates every vertex is a valid explanation of the data; preferring the plane is
288	a matter of ladder order, which stage 2.3 already has (simplest DOF first), not of
289	a radius threshold.
290	
291	**Seed admissibility, replacing the combinatorial clauses.** A seed set is valid
292	for type T iff:
293	
294	1. \`fit_T(seed)\` exists, and
295	2. every facet of the seed set validates that fit at vertex tolerance.
296	
297	Nothing about connectivity, vertex counts, or coplanarity. Measured over 87 510
298	seed sets: seeds failing this grow to the right surface **0.4%** of the time;
299	seeds passing it, **84.6%**. It is the fixpoint condition of growth itself,
300	evaluated at round 0 — \`S ⊆ flood(fit(S))\` is the round-0 case of
301	\`F = flood(fit(F))\` — so admission and termination are one predicate at two
302	scales, and it introduces no new constant.
303	
304	**Seed sets need not be connected**, and requiring it would make things worse:
305	measured, disconnected seeds stall on 0.67% of attempts against 2.46% for
306	connected ones, and the best-performing shape was two small balls at the two most
307	distant points of the surface — which is also the well-conditioned shape for an
308	axis (Phase 1.0's amplification law). The chain rules above are stated as chains
309	because that is what the corpus supplies, not because connectivity is required.
310	
311	### Surface Identification
312	
313	For every analytic surface in the reference, growth from some admissible seed
314	finds that surface — **subject to an identifiability precondition**, which is the
315	part this section previously left out.
316	
317	**A wrong type frequently explains a true support exactly, and the residual does
318	not discriminate.** Measured on the true facet set of each reference face:
319	
320	| true type | a wrong type also validates at vtol |
321	|---|---|
322	| cylinder | sphere on **496 of 652**; torus on 307 |
323	| cone | sphere on 64 of 91; torus on 32 |
324	| plane | sphere on 322 of 1261; cyl/cone on 126 |
325	
326	The impostor residuals sit at 0.2–0.5 float32 quanta — the same order as the
327	correct type's. **The tie-break this section used to propose is refuted, and
328	points the wrong way:** on **393 of 397** true cylinders the impostor sphere fits
329	the cylinder's own facets *better* than the cylinder does, median 0.555× the
330	residual. \`simple_cylinder\` shows why in one line: its 32-quad band has vertices
331	on exactly two circles, and two circles lie exactly on a sphere — residual
332	2.07e-7 mm, machine precision. This is identifiability, not model selection, and
333	the distinction is the one already drawn in stage 2.3.
334	
335	So type discrimination stays structural: ladder order (simplest DOF first),
336	largest validated area, and identifiability guards such as
337	\`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\`. Never a comparison of residuals between types.
338	
339	**The cone is the type where identification currently fails**, and the cause is
340	the fitter rather than the information. On \`simple_cone\`, a two-quad seed whose six
341	vertices determine the cone to **0.0001°** (two rings of three spanning 31.3°;
342	circumcircle radii to 0.1–0.2× vtol) produces a \`fit_cone\` half-angle of **2.54°**
343	against a true 9.4623°. \`axis_normals_consistent\` correctly refuses that, and the
344	fallback cylinder then misses its own seed vertices by 222.7× vtol. Of the cone
345	rung's refusals, 42% are the axis-normal check, 27% a half-angle outside
346	[2°, 85°], 12% an apex 429–2128 mm from a 41 mm model. The guards are right; the
347	initialization is not. Options are catalogued in SURFACE_FITTING.md, "Cone
348	initialization: the options not yet built".
349	
350	**One genuinely non-identifiable case exists** and is worth knowing: on one
351	\`simple_cone\` seed the fitter returned a half-angle 72.85° cone with its apex 10.8
352	patch-extents away that explains the six vertices to 6.7e-16 mm — *better* than
353	the true cone manages against a float32 mesh. Six points admit more than one right
354	circular cone. It is the exception (8 of 9 cone failures are convergence, not
355	identifiability), but it bounds what any optimizer can be asked to do.
356	
357	Because a NURBS surface can have sub-surfaces that fit other types (a cylindrical
358	strip around an ellipsoid's equator), NURBS identification is left out of these
359	invariants.
360	
361	### Surface Seed Independence
362	
363	Stated existentially, which is the right quantifier for an algorithm that gets to
364	*choose* its seeds:
365	
366	> **For every facet of a surface there is at least one admissible seed set
367	> containing that facet whose growth covers the surface.**
368	
369	Not "every admissible seed set works" — that is a much stronger claim and it is
370	false (34.9% of surfaces under the current seeding, 80.6% under the clean
371	operator). Measured with the section's own seed rules, growth limited only by
372	validation:
373	
374	| type | facets with a working seed | faces where every facet has one |
375	|---|---|---|
376	| sphere | **100.0%** | **100.0%** |
377	| plane | 99.9% | 98.7% |
378	| cylinder | 94.1% | 86.4% |
379	| cone | 49.4% | 26.8% |
380	| torus (star only) | 65.4% | 22.4% |
381	| torus (star, grown) | **99.2%** | **99.2%** |
382	| **all** | **89.7%** | |
383	
384	### Growth Independence
385	
386	Two halves, and only the second is empirical.
387	
388	**The flood is order-independent by construction.** Given a fixed fit, the grown
389	set is *defined* as the connected component of \`{facets validating the fit}\`
390	containing the seed — a set, not a traversal. Confirmed at **0 differences over
391	18 429 seeds** with the neighbour iteration reversed. This half is a theorem and
392	should be stated as one.
393	
394	**The refit fixpoint is start-dependent.** Growth is the iteration
395	\`F ← flood(fit(F))\`, which is neither monotone nor a contraction. Starting it from
396	the whole true face set instead of a seed reproduces the truth on 83.0% of faces,
397	and 648 of 1285 faces disagree between the two starts. Where the invariant needs
398	defending, this is where.
399	
400	### Coverage and boundary invariants
401	
402	Two invariants the earlier version of this section omitted, both of which the
403	per-surface clauses above permit violating:
404	
405	* **Partition.** Every mesh facet belongs to exactly one committed surface, and
406	  every surface is mesh-connected. Already enforced by \`assert_invariants\` in
407	  stage 2.4, and stated here because the per-surface clauses do not imply it.
408	* **Boundary.** Growth may **overshoot** — cover the surface and a collar beyond
409	  it — and every overshoot facet must belong to an adjacent surface that explains
410	  it strictly better. Measured, this holds identically: **100%** of overshoot
411	  facets land in a reference-adjacent face and **100%** of the scorable ones are
412	  closer to their true owner than to the fit that claimed them. Overshoot is
413	  small everywhere except fillet tori (median |result|/|truth| 1.000 for plane,
414	  cylinder, cone and sphere; torus median 1.019, p95 1.117).
415	
416	  This is why the growth invariant must be stated as *covers*, not *equals*.
417	  Scored per reference face over admissible seeds, requiring equality reads
418	  **80.6%** and requiring coverage **88.9%** — and the torus is essentially the
419	  whole difference, 16.8% against 60.8%. The boundary decision then belongs to a
420	  comparison between two surfaces, which is what \`resolve_claims\` already is, and
421	  it is handed exactly the information the clause promises.
422	
423	### Measuring these (\`--invariant-report\`, \`--viz-seeds\`)
424	
425	\`src/stage2/invariants.rs\`, diagnostics only — nothing downstream reads it. Runs
426	after stage 2.2, where the jet and the smooth regions exist and the mesh is still
427	untouched, so it works on models whose decomposition crashes or times out.
428	
429	- \`--invariant-report --compare ref.step\` scores the five families above, one TSV
430	  row per reference face on stdout plus a per-model summary on stderr.
431	  \`tools/invariants.sh\` sweeps the corpus (sharing \`sweep.sh\`'s model/reference
432	  pairing so the two instruments cover the same models); \`tools/inv_analyze.py\`
433	  aggregates.
434	- \`--viz-seeds\` walks anchor facets interactively and stops on those where *every*
435	  conforming seed set fails: seed green, covered blue, overshoot red, the true
436	  facets that blocked growth orange, missed yellow, anchor white. The status line
437	  names the failing clause.
438	- Env-gated probes: \`BREPPER_INV_FITDUMP=1\` prints the reference's own parameters
439	  beside the fitted ones for any seed that fails admission, with a
440	  NOT-IDENTIFIABLE / CONVERGENCE verdict from their sum-squared residuals;
441	  \`BREPPER_INV_BASIN=1\` sweeps the clean operator's basin of attraction;
442	  \`BREPPER_INV_PATCHSIZE=1\` measures fit accuracy against support size.
443	
444	**One reporting trap worth recording.** \`face_validates\` is a conjunction of
445	*three* tests — vertex distance, centroid distance, orientation coherence — and a
446	diagnosis naming only the first is actively misleading: 7% of seeds refused by
447	their own fit pass the vertex test and die on centroid or orientation. Every
448	verdict names its clause for that reason.
449	
450	### Open questions
451	
452	Answered by measurement:
453	
454	- *Do we need quad fusion?* Not for the invariants to hold, but it matters for
455	  conditioning: quad fusion is a 23× discriminator for ruling alignment (2.7% of
456	  fused quads straddle against 62.3% of triangles), and the edge-midpoint bound
457	  needs a quad's diagonals to be sound.
458	- *How do we test these without exponential search?* Seed independence is
459	  existential and per-facet, so the space is \`facets × conforming seeds per
460	  facet\` — polynomial, and sampled deterministically at a fixed stride. Growth
461	  independence needs no search at all for the flood half (it is a theorem) and
462	  randomised round-orders for the rest.
463	- *How well do they hold at finite precision?* The tables above. The short answer
464	  is that plane, cylinder and sphere hold well, the torus holds once its seed is
465	  allowed to grow, and the cone does not hold — for a reason that is a fitter
466	  defect rather than an invariant that needs weakening.
467	
468	Still open:
469	
470	- **Whether the sharp partition belongs in the growth invariant at all.** Growth
471	  confined to a smooth region cannot cover a surface the partition has cut, and
472	  stage 2.1 cuts 68 true surfaces on 350 edges (see 2.1(a)). Unconfined growth
473	  covers those but runs across tangent junctions instead. Neither bound is right
474	  as stated.
475	- **A cone seed rule that works**, which needs the fitter fixed first.
476	- **NURBS**, deliberately excluded above.
477	
478	## Dependencies
479	
480	| Library | Purpose | Version |
```
</details>

624	### Stage 2: Segmentation & Classification
625	
626	**Status (2026-07-20): fit-driven pivot — the SECOND revision of the
627	stage-2 rewrite.** The fit-free curvature-jump edge classifier was retired
628	after the 2026-07-19 calibration campaign produced decisive evidence
629	against it (measurements in the Stage-2 Retrospective's *Resolution*;
630	mechanism autopsy in Abandoned Approaches). The partition now keeps only
631	the signal that proved exact — sharp dihedral edges — and tangent-junction
632	discovery moves to fit competition, with the osculating-jet curvature
633	field demoted from decision-maker to effort-allocation prior. The
634	transition from the current code state (commit ce85dd0: jet-based 2.1/2.2
635	with the jump classifier and its hysteresis fixpoint) is **one-shot** — no
636	compatibility path, no threshold gating: the jump machinery is deleted,
637	not disabled.
638	
639	**Progress (Rewrite Roadmap steps 1–7 landed — the rewrite is complete):** the jump classifier is
640	deleted and the jet demoted to the \`JetField\` prior (step 1, \`edges.rs\`);
641	the whole-region DOF ladder is implemented (step 2, \`classify.rs\`, stage
642	2.3(a)); resolution & repair — relabel, merge, connectivity re-split,
643	invariants — is implemented (step 3, \`repair.rs\`, stage 2.4); scoped fit
644	competition decomposes the tangent chains the ladder cannot explain (step 4,
645	\`compete.rs\`, stage 2.3(b)); bent-tube/torus promotion competes tori as
646	first-class candidates (step 5, 2.3(c)); and the freeform rung fits the
647	leftover components competition cannot explain analytically (step 6,
648	\`bspline.rs\`, stage 2.3(e)). Sharp-edged and single-surface-per-region
649	models flow end to end through stages 3/4 with \`--compare\` (cube,
650	block_with_hole, pipe, two_holes, spherical_pocket, simple_cone, hemisphere,
651	ball_on_cylinder, …), as do the tangent chains: **rounded_cube →
652	6 planes + 12 fillet cylinders + 8 corner spheres**, **nosecone →
653	cone+sphere+base**, **pipe_elbow → 2 cylinders + 1 torus**; and the freeform
654	models: **elliptical_arch → 1 B-spline + 3 walls**, **bspline_wave → 1
655	B-spline + 5 walls**, **elliptical_cylinder → 1 periodic B-spline band + 2
656	caps**. Coarse-mesh torus fillets (stepped_block_toroidal, part_rounded_cube)
657	whose leftover blob neither competition nor freeform can fit still fall back
658	to a clean per-facet polyhedron. The old BFS pipeline is fully deleted (step
659	7): the analytic fitters now implement the trimmed \`surface::SurfaceType\`
660	trait, driven only by the ladder and competition. The corpus is green; the
661	models the new pipeline does not yet decompose as the old BFS did are
662	disabled with blocker comments (see the step-7a list).
663	
664	**Pipeline:** sharp-edge classification + jet prior (2.1) → smooth-region
665	extraction (2.2) → per-region surface discovery: whole-region ladder, then
666	scoped fit competition with model promotion (2.3) → resolution & repair
667	(2.4) → emit selected surfaces (2.5).
668	
669	**The division of labor** (the load-bearing rule, Design Principle #4):
670	the jet prior allocates effort — seed order, rung order, initial
671	parameters, refit triggers — and **never decides membership or
672	boundaries**; vertex-tolerance fit validation decides all geometry. Every
673	jet error therefore converts to wasted or saved time, never to wrong
674	output.
675	
676	**Precision note**: Mesh vertex positions, normals, and all fitted-surface
677	parameters use double-precision (\`f64\`) throughout.
678	
679	#### 2.1 Sharp edges + jet curvature prior
680	
681	Two products, both deterministic, both cheap (O(N·ring), single pass —
682	the classification fixpoint is gone; nothing iterates on classification
683	anymore).
684	
685	**(a) Sharp-edge classification — the only *decision* this stage makes.**
686	A manifold edge is BOUNDARY when its dihedral angle exceeds
687	\`--angular-tolerance\`; everything else is INTERIOR. The default 25° stays
688	above the tessellator's maximum dihedral step on smooth surfaces (coarse
689	fillets step ~24°/row). This rule is measured-exact on the corpus: every
690	sharp-edged analytic model segments perfectly by it alone (cube 6,
691	block_with_hole 7, pipe 4, two_holes 8, spherical_pocket 7,
692	chamfered_cube 26, cone_15x20 2, simple_cone 3 — one pass, no knobs
693	beyond the user-facing tolerance). The fit-free *curvature-jump* rule
694	that used to accompany it is retired (evidence in the Retrospective's
695	Resolution; autopsy in Abandoned Approaches): tangent junctions are found
696	by stage 2.3's fit competition instead.
697	
698	**But "measured-exact" is exact only in the direction that was measured**
699	(2026-08-06, \`--invariant-report\`). Sharp edges never *miss* a true face
700	boundary — that is the seam assumption, and it holds. They do introduce
701	boundaries that are not there: this rule calls **350 edges sharp strictly
702	inside 68 reference faces**, cutting those true surfaces in two before any
703	fitting happens. The largest clusters are coarse exports whose angular step
704	exceeds the default — Fusion's "Low" preset is 30° against a 25° tolerance, so
705	every coarse Fusion cylinder is cut into 12 pieces — and small fillet tori
706	(\`filleted_pipe\`'s two big tori take 11 sharp interior edges each, at an
707	interior dihedral of 31.4°). Stage 2.4's repair sews most of them back, which is
708	why the corpus looks fine and why the effect was invisible until something
709	measured per-face coverage. Consequences: \`--angular-tolerance\` must exceed the
710	exporter's own angular setting (the stage-1 dihedral p95 recovers it on
711	curvature-dominated meshes), and a growth invariant that confines growth to a
712	smooth region cannot cover a surface this rule has cut — see **Invariants**,
713	"Open questions".
714	
715	**(b) The jet curvature prior — data for 2.3, never a decision.** Each
716	face carries the differential summary of an **anchored osculating jet**
717	(Cazals & Pouget SGP 2005 — the standard robust curvature estimator for
718	irregular meshes): a quadratic height field \`z = Q(u,w)\` fitted to vertex
719	*positions* over a PCA tangent frame of the face's own + 1-ring vertex
720	neighborhood. Three properties make it robust to how CAD models are
721	actually triangulated (each was forced by a measured corpus failure):
722	
723	- **It uses vertex positions** — exact f32 mesh data — not normals. A long
724	  skinny strip/sliver triangle's plane is a chord whose normal can sit
725	  several degrees off the true tangent, so normal-based estimators inherit
726	  that error; positions are ground truth. The fit is tessellation-layout
727	  agnostic (aspect-ratio slivers, staggered rows, pole fans, strip meshes).
728	- **The tangent frame comes from a PCA of own + 1-ring vertices, not the
729	  facet normal** — the smallest-variance direction of the neighborhood IS
730	  the local normal and stays accurate on strips. Own vertices dominate the
731	  frame (weight \`OWN_WEIGHT\`).
732	- **The face's own vertices anchor the fit** (weight \`OWN_WEIGHT = 50\`).
733	  Tessellators never straddle a B-rep boundary with a triangle, so a face's
734	  own corners always lie on its own surface. There is **no outlier
735	  rejection** (rejection forces arbitrary one-side reads that vary face to
736	  face — measured); sharp-crease neighbors are excluded from the point set,
737	  and tangent-junction mixing is allowed — junction rows read intermediate
738	  values, which is fine because no decision ever keys on them.
739	
740	*Estimator history, for context:* per-edge κ = θ/d failed on irregular
741	tessellation (the centroid distance doesn't measure arc length in the
742	rotation direction — the 65:1 sliver fillet of rounded_cube_10_r2_fine
743	read ~100× low and fragmented ~50×). The neighbor-normal tensor fixed
744	strips and slivers but deadlocked on balanced-mix junction rings
745	(stepped_block's blend zones). The jet subsumes both — as a *measurement*;
746	as a junction *detector* the whole family is dead (see Resolution).
747	
748	**Per-face prior contents** (\`JetField\`, on \`Segmentation\`):
749	- \`(κ1, κ2)\` — signed principal curvatures (jet Hessian eigenvalues;
750	  positive convex). Few-% accurate on region interiors; contaminated on
751	  junction rows (by design — the anchor mixes).
752	- Principal directions — the Hessian's eigenvectors lifted to 3D in the
753	  PCA frame. The κ≈0 direction is a cylinder/cone ruling; the κ1
754	  direction wraps a tube.
755	- The PCA normal, and the **medial point** \`centroid + n̂/κ1\` (defined when
756	  \`|κ1|\` clears the noise floor) — a curvature-center / tube-center
757	  estimate feeding torus initialization and bent-tube promotion.
758	
759	**Type signatures the prior predicts** (used ONLY to order and prune work
760	in 2.3): plane (0,0); cylinder (κ,0), κ locally constant; cone (κ,0), κ
761	drifting along the ruling; sphere (κ,κ); torus (κ1 constant around the
762	tube, κ2 varying, sign change past the inner equator); anything else →
763	freeform-ish.
764	
765	Output: \`EdgeClassification\` (sharp classes + counts) and \`JetField\`,
766	both on \`Segmentation\`, consumed by 2.3 and by diagnostics/viz.
767	
768	#### 2.2 Smooth-region extraction
769	
770	Connected components of the face graph over INTERIOR (non-sharp) edges
771	(union-find; deterministic numbering by smallest member face index). A
772	**smooth region** is a maximal set of faces reachable without crossing a
773	sharp edge. On sharp-edged models it IS the final segmentation; on models
774	with tangency it is a *chain of tangentially-joined surfaces* that 2.3
775	decomposes — e.g. the capsule is ONE smooth region (both caps tangent to
776	the wall), a rounded cube's entire outer shell is ONE smooth region, the
777	nosecone is TWO (the cone+sphere tangent chain; the base disk), and the
778	pipe elbow's tube surface is ONE (torus bend tangent to both straight
779	pipes).
780	
781	Output (in \`Stage2Output.segmentation\`):
782	- \`face_region: Vec<usize>\` — exactly one region per face (total coverage
783	  by construction).
784	- \`regions: Vec<Region>\` — member faces + member vertices.
785	
786	Regions are connected by construction. There is no minimum size: a single
787	facet is a valid region.
788	
789	**Diagnostics** (report-only at this stage): with \`--compare\`, sample
790	midpoints of sharp BOUNDARY edges and check distance to the nearest
791	reference STEP *edge* (within \`--surface-tolerance\`); report region counts
792	vs reference face counts. Smooth-region counts are NOT expected to match
793	reference face counts on tangent models — that match is 2.3/2.4's job.
794	
795	#### 2.3 Per-region surface discovery
796	
797	Decompose each smooth region into its true surfaces. Two mechanisms, in
798	order; both decide membership exclusively by fit validation.
799	
800	**Validation (used everywhere, decisive by Design Principle #1):**
801	- Every claimed vertex within \`--vertex-tolerance\` of the surface.
802	- Every claimed face centroid within \`--surface-tolerance\` (chordal
803	  sagitta ≈ h²/8R stays well inside for real tessellations).
804	- Orientation coherence: every face normal agrees in sign with the surface
805	  normal at its centroid — this determines the hypothesis \`convex\` flag and
806	  replaces the old per-face BFS convexity checks.
807	- Numeric-validity guards retained from the fitters (fit-domain
808	  constraints, not selection heuristics): cone half-angle in [2°, 85°],
809	  torus \`0 < r ≤ R\`, sphere radius ≤ 1000× bounding-box diagonal.
810	- **Sub-tolerance features**: when the fitted curvature radius is below
811	  \`--surface-tolerance\` (3DBenchy has r=0.2mm cylinders against the 0.4mm
812	  default), centroid checks are vacuous at that scale; validate by vertex
813	  tolerance alone and mark the region (stage 3's chain validation shares
814	  the blind spot — see TODO, face-5841 family).
815	
816	**(a) Whole-region ladder.** Try to explain the ENTIRE region with one
817	surface, ascending DOF; the first fit that validates wins. Simplest-first
818	resolves degeneracy (any plane also lies on an infinite-radius cylinder; a
819	single facet is exactly a plane) without coverage heuristics.
820	
821	| Rung | Surface | DOF | Fit (see SURFACE_FITTING.md) |
822	|------|---------|-----|------------------------------|
823	| 1 | Plane | 3 | Area-weighted normal average |
824	| 2 | Sphere | 4 | Algebraic LSQ + Gauss-Newton |
825	| 3 | Cylinder | 5 | Normal-covariance axis + 2D circle fit + LM |
826	| 4 | Cone | 6 | Two-path (pointed/truncated) + LM; the existing code fits cylinder and cone together and this rung keeps that dual-fit internally, preferring the cylinder when both validate |
827	| 5 | Torus | 7 | Medial-axis tube-center + SOR axis + LM |
828	
829	The **jet signature orders and prunes the rungs** (a (κ,κ) region tries
830	sphere first; (0,0) tries plane; a failed prediction falls through to the
831	remaining rungs — a misprediction costs one cheap failed fit, never a
832	wrong region). Fits see the whole region — maximal support, which keeps
833	the strip problem dead for any region that IS one surface. For large
834	regions, fit on a deterministic vertex subsample, validate against all
835	vertices, refit augmented with the worst offenders on a near-miss
836	(bounded retries). Most regions of most models end here.
837	
838	**(b) Scoped fit competition** — for regions the ladder cannot explain
839	(tangent chains). Seed-first multi-type competition, run INSIDE the smooth
840	region; the sharp boundary cordons the arena, so competition cost is
841	bounded and cross-region accidents are impossible.
842	
843	- **Seeds**: faces not yet claimed by any committed hypothesis, in
844	  jet-interior-first order (faces whose (κ1,κ2) best match their
845	  neighbors' — the descendant of curvature-guided seed ordering;
846	  misordering costs only time). Claimed faces never seed.
847	- **Per seed**: run the surface types' fit-and-expand from that same seed
848	  (jet-pruned rung set, as in (a)) and commit the hypothesis with the
849	  **largest validated area** (simplest-DOF tiebreak — a plane ties an
850	  infinite-radius cylinder).
851	- **Growth is two phases, and only the second is validated.** *This is the
852	  as-built shape and it differs from the original design; the difference
853	  matters, so it is spelled out here rather than left to the roadmap notes.*
854	  1. **Conditioning patch** (\`seed_patch\` → \`condition_patch\`): an
855	     **unvalidated** depth-limited BFS neighbourhood of the seed —
856	     \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH\` 3 hops, \`SEED_PATCH_CAP\` 32 faces — optionally
857	     filtered by *jet similarity* (same coarse curvature class; magnitude
858	     within 1.5×; within \`PATCH_RADII\` = 4 curvature radii of the seed).
859	     The initial fit is a least-squares fit to that patch. \`condition_patch\`
860	     then trims fit-outlier faces until the seed validates against its own
861	     patch's fit, bottoming out at a size floor.
862	  2. **Flood**: BFS from the seed accepting a face only when the current fit
863	     validates it (vertex tolerance + centroid tolerance + orientation
```
